作者解释说,如果 Hippo 通路失活,Hippo 通路相关蛋白可以作为 MASH 和 MASH 相关 HCC 的潜在治疗靶点,因为 SH 在 Hippo 不活动的 HCC-M 组 (100%) 中比 HCC-A 组 (50.0%) 更占主导地位,而且 Hippo 不活动组的平均肿瘤大小明显大于 Hippo 活动组。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,针对 Hippo 通路可能是 MASH 和 MASH 相关 HCC 的潜在治疗策略,这可能为未来针对代谢性疾病和 HCC 患者的 治疗 方法和 监测 策略提供信息。
局限与证据强度
该研究受到样本量小(40 个样本)和数据收集的回顾性的限制。由于样本量有限且缺乏前瞻性验证,证据强度中等。

Tumorigenesis of Hepatocellular Carcinoma Related to Metabolic Disorders With a Focus on the Hippo Pathway.

Authors: Haruto Nishida, Keiko Matsuura, Tsutomu Daa
Original abstract (English)
The Hippo pathway has been implicated in the tumorigenesis of hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC). We aimed to determine the expression of Hippo pathway-related proteins in HCC using immunohistochemical staining and compare the data among patients with different underlying clinical background diseases. We collected 40 resected HCC formalin-fixed paraffin-embedded samples and categorized them into 4 groups according to pre-existing conditions [17 samples of alcohol consumption (HCC-A) and 6, 8, and 9 samples with hepatitis B virus infection (HCC-B), hepatitis C virus infection (HCC-C), and metabolic diseases (HCC-M), respectively]. Using immunohistochemistry, we investigated the Hippo pathway-related proteins YAP1, CADM1, and SOX9. Hypertension was the most common disease (72.5%) among patients with HCC, followed by diabetes mellitus (47.5%). Regarding the background liver disease, significant findings of steatohepatitis (SH) were observed in the HCC-A (35.3%) and HCC-M (88.9%) groups compared with those in the HCC-B (16.7%) and HCC-C (0%) groups. A significant finding of metabolic dysfunction-associated SH (MASH) was also observed in the HCC-M group (88.9%), and liver cirrhosis was predominantly observed in the HCC-C group (100%). Twenty-seven patients had Hippo-inactive status, and 13 patients had active status. SH was more predominant in the HCC-M group with Hippo inactivity (100%) than in the HCC-A group (50.0%). The average tumor size was significantly larger in the Hippo-inactive group than in the Hippo-active group. Hippo pathway-associated proteins could serve as potential therapeutic targets for MASH and MASH-related HCC if the Hippo pathway is inactivated.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to determine the expression of Hippo pathway-related proteins in hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) and compare the data among patients with different underlying clinical background diseases, as the Hippo pathway has been implicated in HCC tumorigenesis.
Design and population
We collected 40 resected HCC formalin-fixed paraffin-embedded samples and categorized them into 4 groups according to pre-existing conditions: 17 samples of alcohol consumption (HCC-A), 6 samples with hepatitis B virus infection (HCC-B), 8 samples with hepatitis C virus infection (HCC-C), and 9 samples with metabolic diseases (HCC-M).
Core methods
Using immunohistochemistry, we investigated the Hippo pathway-related proteins YAP1, CADM1, and SOX9 in the collected HCC samples.
Main results
Hypertension was the most common disease (72.5%) among patients with HCC, followed by diabetes mellitus (47.5%). Significant findings of steatohepatitis (SH) were observed in the HCC-A (35.3%) and HCC-M (88.9%) groups compared with those in the HCC-B (16.7%) and HCC-C (0%) groups. A significant finding of metabolic dysfunction-associated SH (MASH) was also observed in the HCC-M group (88.9%), and liver cirrhosis was predominantly observed in the HCC-C group (100%).
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that Hippo pathway-associated proteins could serve as potential therapeutic targets for MASH and MASH-related HCC if the Hippo pathway is inactivated, as SH was more predominant in the HCC-M group with Hippo inactivity (100%) than in the HCC-A group (50.0%), and the average tumor size was significantly larger in the Hippo-inactive group than in the Hippo-active group.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that targeting the Hippo pathway could be a potential therapeutic strategy for MASH and MASH-related HCC, which may inform future treatment approaches and surveillance strategies for patients with metabolic disorders and HCC.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study was limited by the small sample size (40 samples) and the retrospective nature of the data collection. The evidence strength is moderate due to the limited sample size and the lack of prospective validation.

发病率,胃癌后第二原发癌的危险因素和肿瘤谱:一项多中心队列研究长期随访研究。

Incidence, risk factors and tumor spectrum of second primary cancers after gastric cancer: a multicenter cohort study with long-term follow-up.
作者： Xianchun Gao, Weili Han, Linbin Lu, Hongqing Xi, Jun Yu, Ying Liang, Lulu Zhao, Zhijun Tan, Xinrui Li, Zhongyang Zhang
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究解决了胃癌(GC)幸存者中第二原发癌(SPC)定义不明确的 发病率、危险因素和临床影响,旨在量化长期风险并确定相关的临床因素。
设计与对象
这项多中心 队列研究 研究分析了 11,027 名胃癌幸存者(8,519 名男性和 2,508 名女性),他们在 2007 年至 2021 年间在胃综合研究多学科联盟 (MAGIS) 队列研究 内接受了根治性胃切除术。
核心方法
使用 LASSO 惩罚 Cox 回归确定了与 SPC 发生相关的临床因素,并开发了一个探索性的基于风险的分层模型。对无症状 监测 期间检测到的 SPC 与症状出现后诊断的 SPC 进行阶段分布比较。
主要结果
Among 11,027 GC survivors, 241 (2.19%) developed SPCs during up to 16 years of follow-up. The cumulative incidence of SPCs was 1.25% (95% CI, 1.03%-1.46%) at 5 years, 2.97% (95% CI, 2.55%-3.39%) at 10 years, and 4.15% (95% CI, 3.40%-4.91%) at 15 years post-gastrectomy.
解释与创新
该研究发现,SPC 的发生存在明显的性别特异性模式,其中男性以肺癌为主,女性以宫颈癌为主,并证明无症状的 监测 比症状驱动的 诊断 更早地检测到 SPC。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果支持为GC幸存者制定风险适应、器官和性别特异性的监测策略,以改善SPC的早期检测,但还需要在严格的研究中进一步验证。
局限与证据强度
证据因其回顾性设计、对单一 队列研究 的依赖以及风险分层模型缺乏外部验证而受到限制,需要谨慎解释和未来的前瞻性研究。

Incidence, risk factors and tumor spectrum of second primary cancers after gastric cancer: a multicenter cohort study with long-term follow-up.

Authors: Xianchun Gao, Weili Han, Linbin Lu, Hongqing Xi, Jun Yu, Ying Liang, Lulu Zhao, Zhijun Tan, Xinrui Li, Zhongyang Zhang
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: The development of second primary cancers (SPCs) has become an important late effect in gastric cancer (GC) survivors, yet its incidence, risk factors, and clinical implications remain poorly defined. This study aimed to evaluate the long-term risk of SPCs, identify clinical factors associated with SPC occurrence, and describe detection patterns and stage distribution of SPCs during long-term follow-up. METHODS: Patients with GC who underwent curative-intent radical gastrectomy between 2007 and 2021 were identified from the Multidisciplinary Alliance of Gastric Integrative Studies (MAGIS) cohort. Clinical factors associated with SPC occurrence were selected using LASSO-penalized Cox regression, and exploratory risk-based stratification was performed. Stage distribution was descriptively compared between SPCs detected during asymptomatic surveillance and those diagnosed after symptom onset. RESULTS: Among 11,027 GC survivors (8,519 men and 2,508 women), 241 (2.19%) developed SPCs during a follow-up of up to 16 years. The cumulative incidence of SPCs was 1.25% (95% CI, 1.03%-1.46%) at 5 years, 2.97% (95% CI, 2.55%-3.39%) at 10 years, and 4.15% (95% CI, 3.40%-4.91%) at 15 years after gastrectomy. The most frequent SPCs were lung (23.2%), colorectal (21.6%), and esophageal (12.9%) cancers, showing marked sex-specific differences: lung cancer predominated in males, whereas cervical cancer was most common in females. Factors associated with SPC occurrence included older age at time of gastrectomy (HR 1.04, 95% CI 1.02-1.05; p < 0.001), chronic HBV/HCV infection (HR 2.15, 95% CI 1.29-3.58; p = 0.003), family history of cancer (HR 1.57, 95% CI 1.16-2.12; p = 0.003), and elevated preoperative systemic inflammation (Log-SIRI; HR 1.86, 95% CI 1.31-2.65; p < 0.001). The derived model stratified patients into low-, intermediate-, and high-risk groups with corresponding 10-year SPC risks of 2.07%, 3.61%, and 4.33%. SPCs detected during asymptomatic surveillance were more frequently diagnosed at stage I than those identified after symptom onset (47.4% vs 17.6%; p < 0.001). CONCLUSIONS: SPCs represent an important long-term health burden among GC survivors, with distinct sex-specific disease patterns. The exploratory risk-scoring model showed preliminary risk stratification, and SPCs detected during asymptomatic surveillance were more often diagnosed at an earlier stage. These findings support further evaluation of risk-adapted, organ- and sex-specific surveillance strategies in methodologically rigorous studies.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study addressed the poorly defined incidence, risk factors, and clinical implications of second primary cancers (SPCs) among gastric cancer (GC) survivors, aiming to quantify long-term risk and identify associated clinical factors.
Design and population
This multicenter cohort study analyzed 11,027 GC survivors (8,519 men and 2,508 women) who underwent curative-intent radical gastrectomy between 2007 and 2021 within the Multidisciplinary Alliance of Gastric Integrative Studies (MAGIS) cohort.
Core methods
Clinical factors associated with SPC occurrence were identified using LASSO-penalized Cox regression, and an exploratory risk-based stratification model was developed. Stage distribution comparisons were made between SPCs detected during asymptomatic surveillance and those diagnosed after symptom onset.
Main results
Among 11,027 GC survivors, 241 (2.19%) developed SPCs during up to 16 years of follow-up. The cumulative incidence of SPCs was 1.25% (95% CI, 1.03%-1.46%) at 5 years, 2.97% (95% CI, 2.55%-3.39%) at 10 years, and 4.15% (95% CI, 3.40%-4.91%) at 15 years post-gastrectomy.
Interpretation and novelty
The study found distinct sex-specific patterns in SPC occurrence, with lung cancer predominating in males and cervical cancer in females, and demonstrated that asymptomatic surveillance detected SPCs at earlier stages than symptom-driven diagnosis.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings support the development of risk-adapted, organ- and sex-specific surveillance strategies for GC survivors to improve early detection of SPCs, though further validation in rigorous studies is required.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence is limited by its retrospective design, reliance on a single cohort, and lack of external validation for the risk stratification model, warranting cautious interpretation and future prospective studies.

On-治疗 慢性 HBV 患者中 HBV DNA 的微滴数字 PCR 测量:与 HCC 发展和结果的关联。

Droplet Digital PCR Measurement of HBV DNA in On-Treatment Chronic HBV Patients: Association With HCC Development and Outcomes.
作者： Rex Wan-Hin Hui, Danny Ka-Ho Wong, James Fung, Ka-Shing Cheung, Wai-Kay Seto, Lung-Yi Mak, Man-Fung Yuen
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在探讨使用核苷(酸)类似物的 慢性乙型肝炎 患者的低水平残留病毒血症与 HBV 相关肝细胞癌(HCC)发展之间的关系。
设计与对象
该研究是一项回顾性病例对照分析,对 104 名 HCC 患者与 104 名非 HCC 对照进行了比较,这些患者均服用核苷(酸)类似物 (NUC),并且在 HCC 诊断 中通过传统 Cobas-Taqman 检测无法定量血清 HBV DNA。
核心方法
使用经过验证的高灵敏度液滴数字聚合酶链反应测定法测量残留病毒血症,该测定法的检测下限为 1.6 IU/mL。血清样本采集于 HCC 诊断 发生时、诊断 发生前 1 年和 诊断 发生前 2 年。
主要结果
Residual viremia within 2 years before HCC diagnosis was observed in 82.7% of HCC patients and 49.0% of controls (p < 0.001). It was independently associated with HCC occurrence (OR 7.243, 95% CI 1.862-28.170, p = 0.004).
解释与创新
作者解释说,残留病毒血症是 HCC 发生及其进展的一个重要因素,强调需要更灵敏的监测方法。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现表明,高灵敏度 HBV DNA 检测可以改善对 NUC 患者的监测,从而有可能更好地管理 HBV 相关的 HCC 风险。
局限与证据强度
该研究因其回顾性设计和潜在的混杂因素而受到限制,但由于使用了经过验证的高灵敏度测定和明确的统计关联,因此证据充足。

Droplet Digital PCR Measurement of HBV DNA in On-Treatment Chronic HBV Patients: Association With HCC Development and Outcomes.

Authors: Rex Wan-Hin Hui, Danny Ka-Ho Wong, James Fung, Ka-Shing Cheung, Wai-Kay Seto, Lung-Yi Mak, Man-Fung Yuen
Original abstract (English)
Low-level residual viremia can be present in chronic hepatitis B (CHB) patients on nucleos(t)ide analogues (NUCs). Due to the detection limits of current HBV DNA assays, the association between residual viremia and HBV-related HCC has not been well studied. This study included NUC-treated HBV-related HCC patients with unquantifiable serum HBV DNA by the conventional Cobas-Taqman assay (< 20 IU/mL) at HCC diagnosis. The HCC patients were matched in a 1:1 ratio with NUC-treated non-HCC controls. Serum samples at the time of HCC diagnosis, 1 year before, and 2 years before diagnosis were retrospectively retrieved and tested for residual viremia by a validated high-sensitivity droplet digital polymerase chain reaction assay (lower limit of detection 1.6 IU/mL). Among 208 patients (104 HCC vs. 104 controls; mean age 63.1, 80.8% male, 54.3% cirrhosis), residual viremia within 2 years was observed in 82.7% of HCC patients and 49.0% of controls (p < 0.001), and was independently associated with HCC occurrence (OR 7.243, 95% CI 1.862-28.170, p = 0.004). In HCC patients, residual viremia was associated with histological microvascular invasion (44.4% vs. 9.1% in patients without residual viremia, p = 0.033) and lower probability of presenting with Barcelona Clinic Liver Cancer stage 0 HCC (OR 0.281, 95% CI 0.094-0.838, p = 0.023). After median follow-up for 7.2 years, residual viremia within 2 years before HCC diagnosis was independently associated with liver-related mortality (HR 4.472, 95% CI 1.212-16.504, p = 0.025). Residual viremia in NUC-treated CHB patients is associated with a higher risk of HCC development, more advanced tumour staging and poorer outcomes. High-sensitivity HBV DNA assays may be utilized for monitoring in NUC treatment.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study aimed to investigate the association between low-level residual viremia in chronic hepatitis B patients on nucleos(t)ide analogues and the development of HBV-related hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC).
Design and population
The study was a retrospective case-control analysis comparing 104 HCC patients with 104 non-HCC controls who were both on nucleos(t)ide analogues (NUCs) and had unquantifiable serum HBV DNA by the conventional Cobas-Taqman assay at HCC diagnosis.
Core methods
Residual viremia was measured using a validated high-sensitivity droplet digital polymerase chain reaction assay, which had a lower limit of detection of 1.6 IU/mL. Serum samples were collected at the time of HCC diagnosis, 1 year before, and 2 years before diagnosis.
Main results
Residual viremia within 2 years before HCC diagnosis was observed in 82.7% of HCC patients and 49.0% of controls (p < 0.001). It was independently associated with HCC occurrence (OR 7.243, 95% CI 1.862-28.170, p = 0.004).
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that residual viremia is a significant factor in the development of HCC and its progression, highlighting the need for more sensitive monitoring methods.
Scientific and public-health significance
These findings suggest that high-sensitivity HBV DNA assays could improve the monitoring of patients on NUCs, potentially leading to better management of HBV-related HCC risk.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its retrospective design and potential for confounding factors, but the evidence is strong due to the use of a validated high-sensitivity assay and clear statistical associations.

印度尼西亚克服乙型肝炎消除障碍的创新多部门方法:系统文献综述

Innovative Multisectoral Approaches to Overcome Hepatitis B Elimination Barriers in Indonesia: Systematic Literature Review
作者： Nasrullah Tamrin, Awaluddin Awaluddin, Vincentia A. Rizky, Avinda Sari Kanthi, Maria Selviana Joni
摘要中文翻译本研究旨在采用全球循证系统文献综述方法,确定印度尼西亚消除乙型肝炎工作中的主要挑战和多部门策略。本研究的动机是 患病率 的高 慢性乙型肝炎 以及实现世界卫生组织 2030 年消除乙型肝炎目标进展缓慢。在 Scopus 数据库中使用关键词“消除乙型肝炎”、“印度尼西亚”和“多部门挑战”进行文献检索,涵盖2019 年至 2025 年间发表的出版物。分析遵循 PRISMA 2020 指南,包括四个主要阶段:识别、筛选、资格评估和纳入。在最初的 135 篇文章中,有 20 项研究符合标准,并进行了主题分析,以确定趋势、挑战和消除策略。综合确定了三个主要障碍维度:(1) 生物医学,包括对潜伏感染(隐匿性乙型肝炎感染)的有限检测和共价闭合循环的存在脱氧核糖核酸;(2) 卫生系统,包括薄弱的 监测、跨项目协调差和手工报告;(3) 社会,包括耻辱和低 疫苗接种 认识。另一方面,实施数字 监测 系统、加强跨国网络以及高效发展国家 疫苗 等创新战略显示出加速消除乙型肝炎的潜力。本研究强调需要采取综合、跨部门和基于证据的方法来实现印度尼西亚消除乙型肝炎。研究结果为加强乙型肝炎消除做出了概念和实践贡献。全球公共卫生政策,特别是发展中国家的公共卫生政策。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该审查的重点是印度尼西亚消除乙型肝炎的努力,特别确定了主要挑战和多部门战略。范围包括生物医学、卫生系统和社会障碍,以及加速实现世界卫生组织 2030 年消除目标的创新方法。
证据基础与方法
这是遵循 PRISMA 2020 指南的系统文献综述。该搜索是在 Scopus 数据库中进行的,以查找 2019 年至 2025 年间的出版物。在 135 篇初始文章中,有 20 项研究符合标准,并进行了主题分析,以确定趋势、挑战和消除策略。
共识与结论
该综合报告确定了障碍的三个主要方面:生物医学问题,如潜在感染和共价闭合环状 DNA 的有限检测;卫生系统的弱点,包括不良的 监测 和手动报告;以及社会因素,例如耻辱和低 疫苗接种 意识。数字 监测、跨国网络和有效的国家 疫苗 等创新战略显示出加速消除的潜力。
争议与缺口
所提供的证据并未报告具体的争议或证据差距。
科研与实践启示
该研究强调需要采取综合、跨部门和循证的方法来实现印度尼西亚消除乙型肝炎。研究结果有助于加强全球公共卫生政策,特别是发展中国家的公共卫生政策。

Innovative Multisectoral Approaches to Overcome Hepatitis B Elimination Barriers in Indonesia: Systematic Literature Review

Authors: Nasrullah Tamrin, Awaluddin Awaluddin, Vincentia A. Rizky, Avinda Sari Kanthi, Maria Selviana Joni
Original abstract (English)
This study aims to identify the main challenges and multisectoral strategies in hepatitis B elimination efforts in Indonesia, using a global, evidence-based systematic literature review approach.This study was motivated by the high prevalence of chronic hepatitis B and the slow progress in achieving the World Health Organization elimination target of 2030.The literature search was conducted in the Scopus database using the keywords "hepatitis B elimination", "Indonesia", and "multisectoral challenges", covering publications between 2019 and 2025.The analysis followed the PRISMA 2020 guidelines and comprised four main stages: identification, screening, eligibility assessment, and inclusion.Of the initial 135 articles, 20 studies met the criteria and were thematically analyzed to identify trends, challenges, and elimination strategies.The synthesis identified three main dimensions of barriers: (1) biomedical, including limited detection of latent infection (occult hepatitis B infection) and the presence of covalently closed circular DNA; (2) health-system, including weak surveillance, poor cross-program coordination, and manual reporting; and (3) social, including stigma and low vaccination awareness.On the other hand, innovative strategies such as the implementation of digital surveillance systems, the strengthening of cross-country networks, and the development of national vaccines with high effectiveness show the potential to accelerate elimination.This study emphasizes the need for an integrative, cross-sectoral, and evidence-based approach to achieve hepatitis B elimination in Indonesia.The results make conceptual and practical contributions to strengthening global public health policy, particularly in developing countries.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on hepatitis B elimination efforts in Indonesia, specifically identifying main challenges and multisectoral strategies. The scope includes biomedical, health-system, and social barriers, as well as innovative approaches to accelerate progress toward the World Health Organization 2030 elimination target.
Evidence base and method
This is a systematic literature review following PRISMA 2020 guidelines. The search was conducted in the Scopus database for publications between 2019 and 2025. From 135 initial articles, 20 studies met the criteria and underwent thematic analysis to identify trends, challenges, and elimination strategies.
Consensus and conclusions
The synthesis identified three main dimensions of barriers: biomedical issues like limited detection of latent infection and covalently closed circular DNA; health-system weaknesses including poor surveillance and manual reporting; and social factors such as stigma and low vaccination awareness. Innovative strategies like digital surveillance, cross-country networks, and effective national vaccines show potential to accelerate elimination.
Controversies and gaps
The supplied evidence does not report specific controversies or evidence gaps.
Research and practice implications
The study emphasizes the need for an integrative, cross-sectoral, and evidence-based approach to achieve hepatitis B elimination in Indonesia. The results contribute to strengthening global public health policy, particularly in developing countries.

中药对乙型肝炎相关性肝纤维化或肝硬化患者免疫功能的影响:系统评价和荟萃分析

The efficacy of traditional Chinese medicine on immune function in patients with hepatitis B-related liver fibrosis or cirrhosis: a systematic review and meta-analysis
作者： Zhulin Wu, Wanjun Tan, Beier Luo, Xiaowei Liu, Weiqing Zhang, Lianan Wang, Mingbo Lai
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述评估了中药 (TCM) 联合核苷类似物 (NA) 对乙型肝炎相关肝纤维化或肝硬化患者免疫功能的疗效,鉴于 NA 可以控制但很少消除 HBV,因此需要有效的 治疗 来逆转这些状况。
证据基础与方法
这项系统评价和荟萃分析在 PROSPERO 上注册,于 2026 年 4 月 7 日检索了中国国家知识基础设施、万方、SinoMed、Weipu、PubMed、Web of Science、EMBASE 和 Cochrane 数据库。它纳入了 25 项随机对照试验,涉及 2,585 名患者,使用 Cochrane RoB 2 工具评估方法学质量,使用 Review Manager 5.4.1 分析数据,并使用 GRADE 评估证据确定性方法。
共识与结论
Meta分析结果表明,与单独使用NAs相比,中药联合NAs显着改善免疫细胞指标(CD3+、CD4+、CD4+/CD8+、NK细胞),降低CD8+细胞、细胞因子(IL-6、TGF-β、TNF-α)、肝纤维化指标(HA、LN、IV-C、PC-III)、肝脏硬度测量和肝功能指标(ALT、AST、总胆红素、白蛋白)。描述性数据表明中医是安全的。
争议与缺口
偏倚风险评估表明大多数纳入的随机对照试验存在一些担忧。GRADE分级表明免疫功能、肝功能和肝纤维化指标的证据质量较低。漏斗图表明纳入的研究可能存在轻微的发表偏倚。
科研与实践启示
需要更多高质量的随机对照试验来提高中医对该患者群体疗效的证据水平。

The efficacy of traditional Chinese medicine on immune function in patients with hepatitis B-related liver fibrosis or cirrhosis: a systematic review and meta-analysis

Authors: Zhulin Wu, Wanjun Tan, Beier Luo, Xiaowei Liu, Weiqing Zhang, Lianan Wang, Mingbo Lai
Original abstract (English)
Objective Currently, there is no effective treatment to reverse hepatitis B-related liver fibrosis or cirrhosis. Current nucleos(t)ide analogs (NAs) control but rarely eliminate hepatitis B virus (HBV), leaving chronic hepatitis B at risk of developing liver fibrosis/cirrhosis. Traditional Chinese medicine (TCM) shows promise in slowing the progression of hepatitis B-related liver fibrosis/cirrhosis, but its impact on immune function remains debated. Methods The electronic databases, including Chinese National Knowledge Infrastructure, Wanfang, SinoMed, Weipu, PubMed, Web of Science, EMBASE, and Cochrane databases, were retrieved (April 7, 2026), and the randomized controlled trials (RCTs) that met the inclusion criteria were included. Methodologic quality assessment of the included RCTs was done based on the Cochrane RoB 2 tool. Subsequently, the valid data were screened and analyzed by meta-analysis with Review Manager 5.4.1, and the quality of the evidence was assessed using the GRADE method. This study was registered in the PROSPERO (CRD420261351373). Results Finally, 25 RCTs were included, containing 2,585 patients with hepatitis B-related liver fibrosis or cirrhosis. Risk of bias evaluations suggested some concerns for the majority of RCTs. The results of meta-analysis indicated that TCM with NAs could improve CD3 + [MD = 5.83, 95%CI (3.93, 7.73), p < 0.00001], CD4 + [MD = 4.32, 95%CI (3.56, 5.08), p < 0.00001], CD4+/CD8 + [MD = 0.24, 95%CI (0.18, 0.29), p < 0.00001], and NK cells, and decrease CD8 + cells [MD = −2.67, 95%CI (−3.46, −1.89), p < 0.00001], interleukin (IL)-6, transforming growth factor (TGF- β ), tumor necrosis factor- α (TNF-α), hyaluronic acid (HA), laminin (LN), type IV collagen (IV-C), type III procollagen (PC-III), liver stiffness measurement (LSM), alanine aminotransferase (ALT), aspartate aminotransferase (AST), total bilirubin, and albumin in serum compared with NAs alone. Moreover, descriptive data showed that TCM was safe, and the funnel plot suggested the included research might have slight publication bias. The GRADE classification showed that the certainty of evidence was low for the immune function, liver function, and liver fibrosis indexes. Conclusion A combination of NAs and TCM could improve immune cell metrics, cytokines, liver function indexes, and liver fibrosis indexes of patients with hepatitis B-related liver fibrosis or cirrhosis. Due to the low quality of research, more high-quality RCTs are needed to improve the level of evidence. Systematic review registration https://www.crd.york.ac.uk/PROSPERO/view/CRD420261351373 .
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review evaluates the efficacy of Traditional Chinese Medicine (TCM) combined with nucleos(t)ide analogs (NAs) on immune function in patients with hepatitis B-related liver fibrosis or cirrhosis, addressing the need for effective treatments to reverse these conditions given that NAs control but rarely eliminate HBV.
Evidence base and method
This systematic review and meta-analysis, registered in PROSPERO, searched Chinese National Knowledge Infrastructure, Wanfang, SinoMed, Weipu, PubMed, Web of Science, EMBASE, and Cochrane databases on April 7, 2026. It included 25 randomized controlled trials with 2,585 patients, assessed methodologic quality using the Cochrane RoB 2 tool, analyzed data with Review Manager 5.4.1, and evaluated evidence certainty using the GRADE method.
Consensus and conclusions
Meta-analysis results indicated that TCM combined with NAs significantly improved immune cell metrics (CD3+, CD4+, CD4+/CD8+, NK cells) and decreased CD8+ cells, cytokines (IL-6, TGF-β, TNF-α), liver fibrosis indexes (HA, LN, IV-C, PC-III), liver stiffness measurement, and liver function indexes (ALT, AST, total bilirubin, albumin) compared with NAs alone. Descriptive data suggested TCM was safe.
Controversies and gaps
Risk of bias evaluations suggested some concerns for the majority of included RCTs. The GRADE classification indicated that the certainty of evidence was low for immune function, liver function, and liver fibrosis indexes. A funnel plot suggested the included research might have slight publication bias.
Research and practice implications
More high-quality randomized controlled trials are needed to improve the level of evidence regarding the efficacy of TCM in this patient population.

C 补充信息学术文献 · 综述C supplementaryAcademic literature · Review

澳大利亚初级保健诊所对慢性乙型肝炎患者的级联护理监测。

Monitoring of the Cascade of Care in Patients With Chronic HBV in Primary Care Clinics in Australia.
作者： Dina Moussa, Jack Wallace, Jacqui Richmond, Joseph S Doyle, Long Nguyen, Douglas Boyle, Mark A Stoové, Jason Asselin, Victoria Polkinghorne, Marvad Ahad
摘要中文翻译为了在澳大利亚消除 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV),需要从医院护理转向社区护理。本研究旨在描述慢性乙型肝炎的 患病率 以及初级保健患者对级联护理的接受。这项前瞻性 队列研究 研究于 2020 年 1 月 7 日至 2023 年 6 月 30 日在澳大利亚维多利亚州 76 个城市和地区初级保健诊所进行。从全科医生 (GP) 诊所的电子病历 (EMR) 中提取匿名的社会人口统计、临床和实验室数据。对临床 HBV 患者的 队列研究 进行了描述性分析。在整个研究期间,共有 346,927 人参加了预约。其中,25,212 人有 HBV 相关检测记录。491 名 (0.14%) 个体有当前 HBV 感染的证据,定义为 HBV 表面抗原 (HBsAg) 阳性和/或 HBV DNA 阳性。研究期间有 469 人到诊所就诊,其中 239 人(51%)为女性。在 GP EMR 中,只有 59 名患者 (13%) 拥有至少一项 HBV DNA 检测和至少一项 ALT 检测的证据。14 名患者 (3%) 曾接受过诊所护士或全科医生的 HBV 管理检查记录,78 名患者 (17%) 接受过病毒性肝炎专家的检查记录。九十二 (20%) 因 HBV 而接受 治疗 治疗。在接受 治疗 的人中,23 人 (25%) 有与专科护理联系的记录。我们的数据显示,初级保健机构中慢性乙型肝炎患者的级联护理仍存在显着差距,有证据表明参与乙型肝炎级联护理所有阶段的患者比例很低。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本研究重点关注澳大利亚初级保健诊所的 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 感染,旨在描述慢性 HBV 的 患病率 以及患者对级联护理的接受情况。
证据基础与方法
这项前瞻性 队列研究 研究于 2020 年 1 月 7 日至 2023 年 6 月 30 日在澳大利亚维多利亚州 76 个城市和地区初级保健诊所进行,使用来自全科电子病历的匿名社会人口统计、临床和实验室数据。
共识与结论
研究发现,只有 13% 的 HBV 患者有至少一项 HBV DNA 检测和一项 ALT 检测的证据,其中 3% 由临床工作人员进行审查,17% 由病毒性肝炎专家进行审查,20% 接受 治疗,级联护理存在显着差距。
争议与缺口
该研究强调了初级保健机构对慢性乙型肝炎患者的级联护理存在显着差距,参与所有阶段护理的患者比例较低,但没有详细说明具体的争议或未解决的机制。
科研与实践启示
研究结果表明,需要改进初级保健机构中乙肝病毒的监测和管理,以弥补级联护理中的差距,并支持澳大利亚消除乙肝病毒的目标。

Monitoring of the Cascade of Care in Patients With Chronic HBV in Primary Care Clinics in Australia.

Authors: Dina Moussa, Jack Wallace, Jacqui Richmond, Joseph S Doyle, Long Nguyen, Douglas Boyle, Mark A Stoové, Jason Asselin, Victoria Polkinghorne, Marvad Ahad
Original abstract (English)
To achieve hepatitis B virus (HBV) elimination in Australia, a shift from hospital to community-based care is needed. This study aimed to describe the prevalence of chronic HBV and uptake of the cascade of care in primary care patients. This prospective cohort study was conducted in 76 urban and regional primary care clinics across Victoria, Australia between 1/7/2020 and 30/6/2023. Anonymised socio-demographic, clinical and laboratory data from general practice (GP) clinics' electronic medical records (EMR) were extracted. Descriptive analysis of the cohort of clinic clients with HBV was performed. A total of 346,927 individuals attended appointments across the study period. Of these, 25,212 had records indicating HBV-related testing. 491 (0.14%) individuals had evidence of current HBV infection defined as HBV surface antigen (HBsAg) positive and/or HBV DNA positive. 469 attended the clinics during the study period, among whom 239 (51%) were female. In the GP EMR, only 59 (13%) had evidence of at least one HBV DNA test and at least one ALT test ordered. Fourteen patients (3%) had record of being reviewed for HBV management by the clinic nurse or GP and 78 (17%) reviewed by a viral hepatitis specialist. Ninety-two (20%) were on treatment for HBV. Of people receiving treatment, 23 (25%) had a record of linkage to specialist care. Our data show that significant gaps in the cascade of care remain for people with chronic HBV in primary care settings, with a low proportion of patients having evidence of participating in all stages of the HBV cascade of care.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This study focuses on hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection in primary care clinics in Australia, aiming to describe the prevalence of chronic HBV and the uptake of the cascade of care among patients.
Evidence base and method
This prospective cohort study was conducted in 76 urban and regional primary care clinics across Victoria, Australia between 1/7/2020 and 30/6/2023, using anonymised socio-demographic, clinical and laboratory data from general practice electronic medical records.
Consensus and conclusions
The study found that only 13% of patients with HBV had evidence of at least one HBV DNA test and one ALT test ordered, 3% were reviewed by clinic staff, 17% by a viral hepatitis specialist, and 20% were on treatment, with significant gaps in the cascade of care.
Controversies and gaps
The study highlights significant gaps in the cascade of care for people with chronic HBV in primary care settings, with a low proportion of patients participating in all stages of care, though specific controversies or unresolved mechanisms are not detailed.
Research and practice implications
The findings suggest a need for improved monitoring and management of HBV in primary care settings to address the gaps in the cascade of care and support Australia's goal of HBV elimination.

用于短期肝炎进展预测的 HBV 特异性 T 细胞反应:一项可比较、可解释的机器学习研究。

HBV-Specific T Cell Responses for Short-Term Hepatitis Progression Prediction: A Comparative, Interpretable Machine Learning Study.
作者： Sidu Feng, Yandan Wu, Xueyin Mei, Ying Zhou, Tianzhang Zhai, Jian Li, Chuanlai Shen

HBV-Specific T Cell Responses for Short-Term Hepatitis Progression Prediction: A Comparative, Interpretable Machine Learning Study.

乙型肝炎的预防、诊断 和管理:适用于各级护理的医护人员的操作工具包 - ReliefWeb

Prevention, Diagnosis and Management of Hepatitis B: Operational Toolkit for Healthcare Workers Across All Levels of Care - ReliefWeb
新闻简报The Africa CDC has released an operational toolkit for healthcare workers across all levels of care to address hepatitis B in Africa, where the disease burden is disproportionately high. With 64 million people living with chronic hepatitis B in Africa, only 6% had been diagnosed and 1.6% had received treatment by 2024, despite the availability of effective diagnostics and therapy. The toolkit, based on WHO 2024 guidelines, provides practical management algorithms for community, primary care, and hospital settings, accompanied by a mobile app with interactive treatment tools. This initiative aims to improve diagnosis, linkage to care, and treatment uptake to prevent new infections and reduce the estimated 308,000 hepatitis B-related deaths in Africa annually.

规模、影响与风险
An estimated 240 million people globally live with chronic hepatitis B, with Africa accounting for 68% of new infections. In Africa, 6% of PLWHB had been diagnosed and 1.6% had received treatment by 2024, with hepatitis B causing an estimated 308,000 deaths in Africa that year.

A high priorityPublic-health news

Prevention, Diagnosis and Management of Hepatitis B: Operational Toolkit for Healthcare Workers Across All Levels of Care - ReliefWeb

News Brief
The Africa CDC has released an operational toolkit for healthcare workers across all levels of care to address hepatitis B in Africa, where the disease burden is disproportionately high. With 64 million people living with chronic hepatitis B in Africa, only 6% had been diagnosed and 1.6% had received treatment by 2024, despite the availability of effective diagnostics and therapy. The toolkit, based on WHO 2024 guidelines, provides practical management algorithms for community, primary care, and hospital settings, accompanied by a mobile app with interactive treatment tools. This initiative aims to improve diagnosis, linkage to care, and treatment uptake to prevent new infections and reduce the estimated 308,000 hepatitis B-related deaths in Africa annually.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content Help Log in ReliefWeb Search Content Search What are you looking for? Search|t Updates Countries Disasters Organizations Topics Jobs Training Informing humanitarians worldwide 24/7 — a service provided by UN OCHA World Prevention, Diagnosis and Management of Hepatitis B: Operational Toolkit for Healthcare Workers Across All Levels of Care Format Manual and Guideline Source Africa CDC Posted 23 Jul 2026 Originally published 23 Jul 2026 Origin View original Attachments Download Report (PDF | 1.11 MB | Operational guide) Download Report (PDF | 415.62 KB | Reference guide) EXECUTIVE SUMMARY Globally, an estimated 240 million people are living with chronic hepatitis B (PLWHB). The WHO Africa region is disproportionately affected, accounting for 68% of new hepatitis B virus (HBV) infections but having the lowest coverage of timely hepatitis B birth-dose (HepB-BD) vaccination (17%). Of the 64 million PLWHB in Africa, only 6% (3.9 million) had been diagnosed, and just 1.6% (1 million) had received treatment by 2024, despite the availability of accurate WHO prequalified diagnostics and effective, low-cost antiviral therapy. Hepatitis B caused an estimated 308,000 deaths in Africa in 2024. We need to urgently scale up prevention, diagnosis and treatment to prevent new infections and liver-related deaths. In 2024, the WHO African region recorded 612 000 new HBV infections and together with the Western Pacific region accounted for three quarters of the global 1.1 million HBV related deaths. Chronic hepatitis B is a silent disease and is typically asymptomatic until complications such as cirrhosis and hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) develop. It is essential to improve diagnosis, linkage to care, treatment uptake and long term follow up for PLWHB. The operational toolkit should be used alongside the WHO 2024 Hepatitis B Guidelines, the WHO HBV Policy Brief, and the WHO Consolidated Guidance on Hepatitis B and C prevention, testing, treatment, service delivery and monitoring: an implementation handbook for a public health approach. This Africa CDC HBV operational toolkit is based on the WHO 2024 Hepatitis B Guidelines.[2] The toolkit provides practical, easy-to-use management algorithms for healthcare workers across all levels of the health system in Africa, including for those practicing at community, primary care and rural settings. Companion Mobile App This toolkit is accompanied by an App available on the App store for iOS (Apple devices) and the Google Play store for Android devices. The app provides all the materials included in this toolkit and has an interactive treatment advisor to help clinicians determine if treatment is recommended. It includes a calculator for the APRI score and creatinine clearance, and recommendations on doses for renal impairment. Search for “Africa CDC Hepatitis B toolkit” in the App Store or Google Play on your device to download it. In this toolkit, management and referral pathway algorithms are adapted to different levels of care, reflecting the available resources at each level and enabling HBV treatment and care at all levels. Healthcare systems in Africa are stratified into 5 levels based on the resources available for HBV prevention, diagnosis, treatment and monitoring. Each level offers progressively more advanced and comprehensive diagnostic and therapeutic capabilities. Levels of care: Level 0: Community and Outreach Focuses on awareness, basic screening, and referral. Often facilitated by community health workers and outreach programmes. Level 1: Primary Care Provides essential health services including initial screening, basic laboratory tests, and vaccination. Level 2: District Hospital Equipped for basic diagnostics, limited imaging, and some specialised services. Level 3: Regional Hospital Offers more advanced diagnostics and imaging, and specialist services. Level 4: Tertiary Level Hospital/Academic Centres Provides comprehensive diagnostics, advanced imaging, specialised care, research and training. Time
Published on 23 Jul 2026, with data from 2024.
Location and population
Africa region, with 64 million people living with chronic hepatitis B (PLWHB).
Event
Release of an operational toolkit for healthcare workers across all levels of care to prevent, diagnose, and manage hepatitis B, based on WHO 2024 guidelines.
Scale, impact and risk
An estimated 240 million people globally live with chronic hepatitis B, with Africa accounting for 68% of new infections. In Africa, 6% of PLWHB had been diagnosed and 1.6% had received treatment by 2024, with hepatitis B causing an estimated 308,000 deaths in Africa that year.
Response, status and uncertainty
The toolkit provides practical management algorithms adapted to five levels of care in Africa, from community to tertiary hospitals, and includes a companion mobile app with interactive treatment advisors. The response aims to scale up prevention, diagnosis, and treatment to reduce new infections and liver-related deaths.

关于世界肝炎日 |病毒性肝炎意识 - 疾病控制和预防中心 |疾病预防控制中心 (.gov)

About World Hepatitis Day | Viral Hepatitis Awareness - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)
新闻简报World Hepatitis Day, observed annually on July 28, is a global awareness campaign led by the CDC and other organizations to combat viral hepatitis, which affects nearly 300 million people worldwide and causes approximately 1.3 million deaths each year. Despite available vaccines and treatments, hepatitis-related deaths are rising. The CDC collaborates internationally to eliminate hepatitis as a public health threat, supporting countries in building surveillance and treatment capacity. Achieving global vaccination targets could prevent nearly 900,000 hepatitis B infections and 67,000 deaths in the U.S. by 2070, saving $8.1 billion. The agency also promotes innovative testing strategies, such as point-of-care and self-testing, lessons from which are applied domestically to advance hepatitis C elimination.
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 21 日。每年 7 月 28 日为世界肝炎日。
地点与对象
全球性,特别关注美国以及与感染率高的国家的国际合作,包括格鲁吉亚、吉尔吉斯共和国、乌兹别克斯坦、马拉维和博茨瓦纳。
事件
世界肝炎日宣传活动,得到疾病预防控制中心和全球其他组织的认可,旨在提高人们对病毒性肝炎、其负担、持续抗击病毒的努力以及预防行动的认识。该活动已得到确认并具有历史意义,是为了纪念巴鲁克·布隆伯格博士的生日而设立的。
规模、影响与风险
Viral hepatitis affects nearly 300 million people globally, with approximately 1.3 million deaths each year from hepatitis B and hepatitis C. Despite effective vaccines and medications, deaths from viral hepatitis are increasing globally. CDC estimates that achieving global 2030 vaccination targets could prevent almost 900,000 hepatitis B virus infections and over 67,000 deaths in the United States alone.
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC 正在努力消除美国和全球的病毒性肝炎,与国际合作伙伴合作建设 监测、检测、护理和 治疗 的能力。该机构支持多个国家的国家血清调查,并监督乌兹别克斯坦指南的实施情况。全球工作提供了现场护理测试和自我测试策略的例子。来源为CDC官方网站,提供权威信息,可信度高。
A high priorityPublic-health news

About World Hepatitis Day | Viral Hepatitis Awareness - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)

News Brief
World Hepatitis Day, observed annually on July 28, is a global awareness campaign led by the CDC and other organizations to combat viral hepatitis, which affects nearly 300 million people worldwide and causes approximately 1.3 million deaths each year. Despite available vaccines and treatments, hepatitis-related deaths are rising. The CDC collaborates internationally to eliminate hepatitis as a public health threat, supporting countries in building surveillance and treatment capacity. Achieving global vaccination targets could prevent nearly 900,000 hepatitis B infections and 67,000 deaths in the U.S. by 2070, saving $8.1 billion. The agency also promotes innovative testing strategies, such as point-of-care and self-testing, lessons from which are applied domestically to advance hepatitis C elimination.

Viral Hepatitis Awareness Explore This Topic Search Search Clear Search For Everyone Viral Hepatitis Awareness Month and National Hepatitis Testing Day Social Media Toolkit ABCs of Viral Hepatitis View all search clear search Viral Hepatitis Awareness Menu clear search For Everyone Viral Hepatitis Awareness Month and National Hepatitis Testing Day Social Media Toolkit ABCs of Viral Hepatitis View All Viral Hepatitis Awareness Month and National Hepatitis Testing Day Social Media Toolkit ABCs of Viral Hepatitis View Menu About World Hepatitis Day For Everyone July 21, 2026 What to know July 28 is World Hepatitis Day (WHD). Why it's important Viral hepatitis affects nearly 300 million people globally, with approximately 1.3 million deaths each year from hepatitis B and hepatitis C. Despite effective vaccines, evidence-based prevention strategies and medications, deaths from viral hepatitis are increasing globally. CDC and other organizations around the world recognize WHD by raising awareness about viral hepatitis, the burdens people with hepatitis face, ongoing work to combat viral hepatitis across the globe, and actions people can take to prevent future transmission. Goals CDC is working to eliminate viral hepatitis in the United States and around the world. CDC collaborates with international partners to help countries experiencing high rates of infection to prevent, control, and eliminate viral hepatitis as a global public health threat. Campaign origins WHD is recognized annually on July 28, the birthday of Dr. Baruch Blumberg, who discovered the hepatitis B virus in 1967. Two years later, he developed the first hepatitis B vaccine. Dr. Blumberg won the Nobel Prize in Physiology or Medicine in 1976. What success looks like CDC provides scientific and public health leadership in the United States and to countries around the world in advancing efforts to eliminate viral hepatitis as a public health threat. CDC helps countries build capacity for viral hepatitis surveillance, testing, care, and treatment and assists with development and implementation of national control and elimination programs. CDC's global viral hepatitis work protects the United States. Persons who were not born in the United States account for 3 out of 4 people with hepatitis B virus infections. Simulation studies suggest that since 2000, progress in expanding global hepatitis B vaccination among all countries with people who immigrate to the United States could prevent almost 900,000 hepatitis B virus infections and over 67,000 hepatitis B-related deaths if global 2030 vaccination targets are achieved and sustained; this would save the United States $8.1 billion in estimated costs by 2070. CDC's support to other countries makes America stronger by fortifying international partnerships, advancing innovation, and using lessons from other countries to advance the elimination of hepatitis C in the United States. Notably, global work has provided examples of the usefulness for tests at point-of-care, such as a viral test for hepatitis C diagnosis that can be used to link people to care and curative treatment in real time. Evaluations of use of hepatitis C self-testing (i.e. for antibody to hepatitis C virus) has shown strong usability and acceptance in the country of Georgia providing lessons for potential use in other low- and middle-income countries to identify persons exposed to hepatitis C who do not typically access health care services. CDC has supported the first national viral hepatitis serosurveys to estimate the burden of hepatitis B, hepatitis C, and hepatitis D in Georgia, the Kyrgyz Republic, Uzbekistan, Malawi, and Botswana, providing data that guide the development of national strategic plans, identify strategic priorities needed to achieve elimination of viral hepatitis, and measure progress toward the elimination of hepatitis B and hepatitis C. To learn more about how CDC is working globally to eliminate global death and disease caused by viral hepatitis, see CDC Collaborates Worldwide to Eliminate Viral Hepatitis. CDC conducting a site visit to monitor implementation of newly developed guidelines to prevent and manage hepatitis B and hepatitis C in hemodialysis and hematology-oncology clinics in Uzbekistan. Resources For people looking for general information on viral hepatitis, see: Hepatitis A Basics Learn the basics of hepatitis A, including symptoms, spread, prevention, vaccination, and treatment. Hepatitis D Basics Learn about hepatitis D testing, symptoms, transmission, prevention, diagnosis and treatment. Hepatitis E Basics Discover hepatitis E: a liver infection by HEV. View five news elements
Time
Published on July 21, 2026. World Hepatitis Day is recognized annually on July 28.
Location and population
Global, with specific focus on the United States and international collaboration with countries experiencing high rates of infection, including Georgia, the Kyrgyz Republic, Uzbekistan, Malawi, and Botswana.
Event
World Hepatitis Day awareness campaign, recognized by CDC and other organizations globally to raise awareness about viral hepatitis, its burdens, ongoing efforts to combat it, and prevention actions. The event is confirmed and historical, established in honor of Dr. Baruch Blumberg's birthday.
Scale, impact and risk
Viral hepatitis affects nearly 300 million people globally, with approximately 1.3 million deaths each year from hepatitis B and hepatitis C. Despite effective vaccines and medications, deaths from viral hepatitis are increasing globally. CDC estimates that achieving global 2030 vaccination targets could prevent almost 900,000 hepatitis B virus infections and over 67,000 deaths in the United States alone.
Response, status and uncertainty
CDC is working to eliminate viral hepatitis in the U.S. and globally, collaborating with international partners to build capacity for surveillance, testing, care, and treatment. The agency supports national serosurveys in multiple countries and monitors implementation of guidelines in Uzbekistan. Global work has provided examples of point-of-care tests and self-testing strategies. The source is an official CDC website, providing authoritative information with high confidence.

新闻简报最近对路德维希·范·贝多芬头发的基因研究揭示了有关他的健康和血统的新见解。研究人员分析了作曲家的八把锁,其中五个样本通过基因兼容性确认为真实的。测序揭示了他患有肝病的遗传倾向,以及他在 1827 年去世前感染乙型肝炎的证据。然而,一个重大发现是,长期与贝多芬相关并在之前的研究中使用的著名的希勒锁实际上属于一位女性。这一发现使之前仅根据该样本得出的结论无效。这项发表在《当代生物学》上的研究强调了基因鉴定在历史研究中的重要性。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Hair attributed to Beethoven undergoes genetic sequencing and reveals risk of liver disease, hepatitis B, and a family secret, but one of the most famous locks kept for almost 200 years wasn't even his and belonged to a woman. - CPG Click Oil and Gas

News Brief
A recent genetic study of Ludwig van Beethoven's hair has uncovered new insights into his health and ancestry. Researchers analyzed eight locks attributed to the composer, with five samples confirmed as authentic through genetic compatibility. The sequencing revealed a genetic predisposition to liver disease and evidence of hepatitis B infection before his death in 1827. However, a significant discovery was that the famous Hiller Lock, long associated with Beethoven and used in previous studies, actually belonged to a woman. This finding invalidates earlier conclusions based solely on this sample. The study, published in Current Biology, highlights the importance of genetic authentication in historical research.

Leave a comment 6 min of reading Hair attributed to Beethoven undergoes genetic sequencing and reveals risk of liver disease, hepatitis B, and a family secret, but one of the most famous locks kept for almost 200 years wasn’t even his and belonged to a woman. Written by Valdemar Medeiros Published on 23/07/2026 at 09:23 Science and Technology Follow us on Google News Be the first to react! 0 0 0 0 0 0 Prefer CPG on Google Listen to this content Researchers analyzed eight hair locks attributed to Ludwig van Beethoven and managed to sequence the composer’s genome from five samples considered authentic. The study revealed a genetic predisposition to liver disease, signs of hepatitis B infection before death, and a historical surprise: the famous Hiller lock, used in previous lead studies, did not belong to Beethoven but to a woman. Ludwig van Beethoven died in 1827, at the age of 56, after suffering for decades from progressive hearing loss, gastrointestinal problems, and severe liver disease. Almost 200 years later, scientists decided to investigate part of this mystery using DNA preserved in hair locks attributed to the composer. The study, published in the scientific journal Current Biology, analyzed eight independent locks attributed to Beethoven. Five of them showed genetic compatibility with each other and were considered almost certainly authentic by the researchers. The analysis revealed a genetic predisposition to liver disease, evidence of hepatitis B infection, and an unexpected discovery about the composer’s family tree. According to University of Cambridge, one of the most famous locks, known as the Hiller Lock, was not from Beethoven but from a woman. ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW See also Small chip implanted behind the eye connects to glasses with a camera, transforms images into visual stimuli, and can restore part of the vision lost due to macular degeneration. Without a car, an engineering student builds an electric wooden motorcycle from cedar parts equipped with a 2,000 W motor, hydraulic brakes, and lighting, and puts the vehicle to work on the streets. Arctic sea ice experiences the largest annual drop of the winter peak phase since the beginning of satellite measurements, losing 5.8% of its extent between 2024 and 2025, ending the apparent pause that concealed the ongoing reduction in a region crucial for the climate. Scientists discover a surprising “brain” hidden in the heart, formed by neurons capable of controlling the heartbeat and protecting the organ during stressful situations. The study analyzed eight locks attributed to Beethoven The research examined eight hair samples historically associated with Beethoven. The goal was to authenticate the material, sequence the composer’s genome, and search for clues about diseases that marked his life. According to the article published in Current Biology, five locks came from the same European man and were considered compatible with Beethoven’s documented origin. These samples allowed for a deeper genomic analysis. The best-preserved sample was the so-called Stumpff Lock. From it, scientists sequenced Beethoven’s nuclear genome with an average coverage of 24 times, a sufficient level to investigate ancestry, genetic risks, and signs of ancient pathogens. The most famous lock was not from Beethoven The biggest turnaround in the study involved the so-called Hiller Lock, a lock of hair traditionally associated with the composer and supposedly cut the day after his death. It had already been used in previous analyses that reinforced hypotheses about lead poisoning. CREDIT: KEVIN BROWN / AMERICAN BEETHOVEN SOCIETY The new sequencing showed that this lock did not belong to Beethoven. According to the University of Cambridge, the material came from a woman, not the German composer. This discovery weakens old conclusions based solely on this sample. As explained by the Beethoven-Haus Bonn, previous analyses done exclusively on the Hiller Lock can no longer be directly applied to Beethoven. Sequencing revealed genetic risk for liver disease The study found in Beethoven a relevant genetic predisposition for liver disease. The analysis identified variants associated with a higher risk of progressive liver problems. According to the University of Cambridge repository, Beethoven was homozygous for a variant in the PNPLA3 gene, strongly associated with progressive liver disease. He also presented variants in the HFE gene, related to iron metabolism. These data alone do not prove the cause of death. They indicate that Beethoven had genetic factors that could increase liver vulnerability, especially when combined with other risk elements. Hepatitis B appeared in the genetic material In addition to hereditary predisposition, researchers found evidence of hepatitis B infection. The virus was detected in metagenomic analyses made from the samples considered authentic. According to the Max Planck record, the data indicate that Beethoven had a hepatitis B infection during at least the months prior to his death. This information is important because hepatitis B can cause chronic inflammation in the liver and worsen hepatic conditions. In Beethoven’s case, researchers point out that the infection may have added to the genetic predisposition and alcohol consumption. Alcohol may have worsened the composer’s condition Historical accounts indicate that Beethoven regularly consumed alcohol, although the exact amount remains a subject of debate. The genetic study does not directly measure how much he drank. The University of Cambridge stated that the combination of genetic predisposition, possible alcohol consumption, and hepatitis B infection offers a plausible explanation for the composer’s severe liver disease. Nevertheless, the authors do not claim that alcohol alone killed Beethoven. The central point is that the composer’s liver had multiple risk factors, making the cause of death more complex than a single explanation. DNA did not explain Beethoven’s deafness Beethoven began losing his hearing at a young age and eventually became almost completely deaf. This has always been one of the greatest medical mysteries associated with the composer. Even with genetic sequencing, scientists did not find a definitive explanation for the deafness. The Current Biology article states that the study failed to identify a genetic cause for the hearing problems. The same goes for the chronic gastrointestinal complaints. The DNA provided important clues about the liver but did not solve all the health problems described in letters and historical accounts. Family secret appeared on the Y chromosome The study also compared Beethoven’s Y chromosome with that of modern men who carry the Beethoven surname and descend from the documented paternal lineage of the family. The analysis revealed a mismatch between the composer’s Y chromosome and that of the five modern relatives studied. According to the University of Cambridge, this indicates an extramarital paternity event at some point in the paternal lineage. This event would have occurred between the conception of Hendrik van Beethoven in the 16th century and that of Ludwig van Beethoven in 1770. The study does not identify in which generation this happened. Result changed previous studies on lead For years, one of the most popular hypotheses about Beethoven’s health involved lead poisoning. Part of this narrative was constructed from analyses done on the Hiller Lock. Since this lock has now been identified as belonging to a woman, the results based solely on it cannot be used as direct evidence about Beethoven’s body. The Max Planck highlighted this consequence when releasing the study. This does not mean that all investigation into lead is closed. It only means that one of the most famous samples, used to support this theory, did not genetically represent the composer. Study used hair because bones were not available The work did not analyze bone remains directly associated with Beethoven. Researchers turned to locks preserved in public and private collections, many of them accompanied by historical documents of provenance. Genetic authentication was crucial to separate compatible samples from materials incorrectly attributed to the composer. The article in Current Biology reports that five of the eight locks analyzed belonged to the same European man. This procedure shows how paleogenomics can correct errors accumulated in historical collections. An object kept for decades with a famous attribution can completely lose its biographical value when subjected to genetic testing. Research did not pinpoint a single cause for the death Beethoven died in Vienna on March 26, 1827. Historical documents describe symptoms compatible with advanced liver disease, but the medicine of the time did not have the necessary resources for a modern diagnosis. The new study reinforces that the composer had a genetic predisposition to liver problems and also evidence of hepatitis B infection. These factors may have contributed to the final illness. Even so, the exact cause of death was not conclusively determined. The most reliable result is that Beethoven’s liver disease likely involved a combination of genetic risk, viral infection, and environmental factors. Genetics reopened a historical case of almost 200 years The case shows how objects preserved for generations can gain new interpretations when analyzed with modern technologies. A simple lock of hair began to reveal data about health, ancestry, and errors in historical attribution. The strongest discovery was not just finding Beethoven’s DNA. It was separating what truly came from him from what had been associated with the composer by tradition, incomplete documentation, or historical error. Almost two centuries after his death, Beethoven remains surrounded by questions. The genome did not explain all the mysteries, but it showed that the composer’s medical history was more complex than old theories suggested. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

健康运动聚焦各年龄段乙型肝炎预防 - 中国日报

Health campaign spotlights hepatitis B prevention across all ages - China Daily
新闻简报China is intensifying efforts to eliminate hepatitis B as a public health threat by 2030, with a health campaign in Beijing bringing together officials and clinicians to assess progress. Despite significant achievements—such as 99.6% vaccination coverage for infants and a 0.30% prevalence rate among children under five—75 million people aged 1 to 69 remain chronically infected, accounting for one-third of global cases. A large portion are unaware of their status, and the virus poses risks of cirrhosis and liver cancer. The 2025-2030 action plan focuses on maintaining high childhood vaccination rates while expanding adult coverage, with grassroots health centers playing a key role in education and screening. Experts emphasize prompt newborn vaccination, antibody monitoring for children, and reducing stigma to encourage preventive measures among families.
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于2026-07-19;事件发生在同一天,北京,当时正值卫生活动期间。
地点与对象
中国北京;受影响人群包括1至69岁的慢性乙型肝炎感染者,特别关注婴儿、儿童和成人。
事件
确认到 2030 年开展公共卫生运动,控制和消除乙型肝炎这一公共卫生威胁;官员和临床医生齐聚一堂,评估进展并规划下一步行动。
规模、影响与风险
An estimated 75 million people aged 1 to 69 are living with chronic HBV infection, accounting for roughly one-third of global cases; prevalence among children under five is 0.30%, and among the broader population aged 1 to 69 is 5.86%; significant proportion unaware of status; virus is highly contagious and can lead to cirrhosis and liver cancer.
应对、状态与不确定性
2005年推出通用免费婴儿疫苗接种,三剂系列覆盖率达99.6%,及时出生剂覆盖率达95.6%;发布2025-2030年行动计划,维持较高的儿童疫苗接种率并扩大成人免疫接种;基层卫生院在健康教育、筛查、随访等方面发挥着重要作用;持续努力减少家庭成员的耻辱并鼓励采取预防措施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Health campaign spotlights hepatitis B prevention across all ages - China Daily

News Brief
China is intensifying efforts to eliminate hepatitis B as a public health threat by 2030, with a health campaign in Beijing bringing together officials and clinicians to assess progress. Despite significant achievements—such as 99.6% vaccination coverage for infants and a 0.30% prevalence rate among children under five—75 million people aged 1 to 69 remain chronically infected, accounting for one-third of global cases. A large portion are unaware of their status, and the virus poses risks of cirrhosis and liver cancer. The 2025-2030 action plan focuses on maintaining high childhood vaccination rates while expanding adult coverage, with grassroots health centers playing a key role in education and screening. Experts emphasize prompt newborn vaccination, antibody monitoring for children, and reducing stigma to encourage preventive measures among families.
Original source text (English)
Search HOME CHINA WORLD BUSINESS CULTURE TRAVEL VIDEO SPORTS OPINION REGIONAL NEWSPAPER MOBILE Global EditionASIA 中文双语Français HOME CHINA WORLD BUSINESS CULTURE TRAVEL VIDEO SPORTS OPINION REGIONAL NEWSPAPER China Daily PDF China Daily E-paper MOBILE China News Society Innovation HK/Macao Cross-Strait Cover Story Photo Environment Health Military Video Home / China / Society Home China Society Health campaign spotlights hepatitis B prevention across all ages chinadaily.com.cn | Updated: 2026-07-19 18:38 Share Share - WeChat CLOSE Runners are seen during a health campaign to control and eliminate hepatitis B in Beijing on Saturday. [Photo provided to chinadaily.com.cn] With four years remaining until the 2030 target for eliminating viral hepatitis as a public health threat domestically, public health officials and clinicians gathered in Beijing to assess progress and map out next steps in hepatitis B control. Hepatitis B remains a major public health challenge in China. An estimated 75 million people aged 1 to 69 are living with chronic HBV infection, accounting for roughly one-third of global cases. A significant proportion of those infected are unaware of their status. The virus is highly contagious, and chronic infection can progress to cirrhosis and liver cancer. Since the introduction of universal free infant vaccination in 2005, coverage rates have climbed to 99.6% for the three-dose series and 95.6% for the timely birth dose—well above the global average of 85%. As a result, the prevalence of hepatitis B surface antigen among children under five has dropped to 0.30%, down from much higher levels in the 1990s. Among the broader population aged 1 to 69, the prevalence now stands at 5.86%. These achievements do not mark the finish line. In September 2025, the National Disease Control and Prevention Administration and eight other government departments jointly issued an action plan for As part of the 2025–2030 action plan for addressing the disease, efforts to maintain high childhood vaccination rates while expanding adult immunization coverage are in full swing. Yao Fengli, deputy chief pediatrician at the Fifth Medical Center of the People's Liberation Army General Hospital, said that infants infected with HBV face a 90% risk of developing chronic infection, with long-term risks of cirrhosis and liver cancer. She urged that newborns should receive their first vaccine dose promptly after birth, followed by doses at one month and six months of age. As children enter preschool and school settings, group activities and close contact increase the risk of horizontal transmission. Parents should monitor their children's antibody levels and consult healthcare providers about screening and booster doses when necessary. Lyv Sa, senior physician at the Fifth Medical Center of the People's Liberation Army General Hospital’s liver disease department, said that HBV is not transmitted through everyday contact such as handshakes, hugging, shared meals, or attending classes together. She called for reducing stigma and encouraging family members of those infected to know their immune status and take appropriate preventive measures. Zhang Ying, chief physician at the Tianjin CDC, said that grassroots health centers play a critical role in delivering health education, on-site screening, vaccination reminders, and standardized follow-up care. Phase 3 results for hepatitis B treatment show functional cure for 1 in 5 people

Phase 3 results for hepatitis B treatment show functional cure for 1 in 5 people
新闻简报Phase 3 clinical trials of bepirovirsen, an antisense oligonucleotide treatment, have demonstrated a functional cure in approximately 20% of adults with chronic hepatitis B, marking a significant advancement in the field. The trials, conducted across 29 countries with over 1,800 participants, showed that a finite course of bepirovirsen, combined with background nucleotide analogue therapy, enabled sustained loss of HBsAg and undetectable HBV DNA in 20% of treated patients, compared to 0% in the placebo group. Higher cure rates (26%) were observed in patients with lower baseline HBsAg levels. While these results offer hope for reducing lifelong treatment dependency, further research is needed to improve response rates, assess long-term durability, and explore combination therapies. Chronic hepatitis B remains a major global health challenge, affecting an estimated 250 million people worldwide.
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-07-22。事件是指2022年至2025年期间进行的3期临床试验,随访至第72周。
地点与对象
超过 1,800 名患有非肝硬化 慢性乙型肝炎 的成人接受核苷酸类似物 (NA) 治疗 至少 6 个月。
事件
贝匹罗韦森(一种反义寡核苷酸 (ASO) 治疗)的 3 期临床试验(B-Well 1 和 B-Well 2)显示,约 20% 的 慢性乙型肝炎 患者出现 功能性治愈,定义为 治疗 治疗后 24 周后 HBsAg 持续消失且 HBV DNA 检测不到。
规模、影响与风险
Functional cure achieved in 20% of bepirovirsen recipients (0% in placebo). Higher cure rates (26%) observed in patients with lower baseline HBsAg levels (<1,000 IU/ml). Chronic hepatitis B affects an estimated 250 million people globally, predisposing them to cirrhosis, liver failure, and liver cancer.
应对、状态与不确定性
治疗 涉及 24 周的皮下贝匹罗韦森注射加背景 NA 治疗,然后继续仅使用 NA。需要进一步的研究来提高反应率、评估长期耐久性并评估更广泛的人群。关于更广泛的患者群体的结果和潜在的联合疗法仍然存在不确定性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Phase 3 results for hepatitis B treatment show functional cure for 1 in 5 people

News Brief
Phase 3 clinical trials of bepirovirsen, an antisense oligonucleotide treatment, have demonstrated a functional cure in approximately 20% of adults with chronic hepatitis B, marking a significant advancement in the field. The trials, conducted across 29 countries with over 1,800 participants, showed that a finite course of bepirovirsen, combined with background nucleotide analogue therapy, enabled sustained loss of HBsAg and undetectable HBV DNA in 20% of treated patients, compared to 0% in the placebo group. Higher cure rates (26%) were observed in patients with lower baseline HBsAg levels. While these results offer hope for reducing lifelong treatment dependency, further research is needed to improve response rates, assess long-term durability, and explore combination therapies. Chronic hepatitis B remains a major global health challenge, affecting an estimated 250 million people worldwide.
Original source text (English)
Share on Pinterest Phase 3 trials suggest bepirovirsen can achieve a functional cure in about one in five people with hepatitis B. Image credit: Getty Images / Liliya Krueger In two phase 3 clinical trials, roughly one in five adults with chronic hepatitis B achieved sustained loss of hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) and undetectable hepatitis B viral DNA after completing a finite course of bepirovirsen treatment. Bepirovirsen reduces HBsAg production, aiming to restore the immune system’s ability to control the infection rather than simply suppressing viral replication. Participants with lower baseline HBsAg levels experienced higher functional cure rates, suggesting patient selection may play an important role in treatment success. While the results represent promising steps toward a functional cure for chronic hepatitis B, further research is necessary to improve response rates, evaluate long-term durability, and determine how the therapy performs in broader patient populations. Chronic hepatitis B is a viral infection that affects the liver and remains a major global health challenge . Estimates suggest that roughly 250 million people are living with chronic hepatitis B, which predisposes them to develop severe complications, such as cirrhosis , liver failure , and liver cancer . Current first-line treatment will typically involve antiviral therapy to suppress viral replication. However, these drugs rarely eliminate hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg), which is a key marker of persistent infection. Thus, many individuals may need to continue treatment indefinitely. As a result, researchers have increasingly focused on therapies capable of achieving a functional cure , defined as sustained loss of HBsAg and undetectable HBV DNA after treatment has ended. Now, results from two large phase 3 clinical trials indicate that the investigational drug bepirovirsen could mark a significant step toward a functional cure for chronic hepatitis B, offering hope beyond lifelong antiviral treatment for some patients. The findings, published in the New England Journal of Medicine , showed that approximately 20% of eligible patients achieved a sustained functional cure after completing a finite course of treatment, an outcome not observed in those receiving a placebo. What did the phase 3 trials find? The phase 3 B-Well 1 and B-Well 2 studies enrolled more than 1,800 adults with non-cirrhotic chronic hepatitis B across 29 countries, who had been receiving nucleotide analogue (NA) treatment for at least 6 months between 2022 and 2025. Participants were randomly assigned 2:1 to receive either 300 milligrams (mg) subcutaneous injections of bepirovirsen or a placebo weekly for 24 weeks, in addition to background NA therapy, followed by an additional 24 weeks of NA treatment. All participants had an HBsAg level of more than 100 to 3000 international units per milliliter (IU/ml). Eligible individuals discontinued NA therapy at 48 weeks. The primary outcome was a functional cure at week 72, defined as maintaining undetectable hepatitis B viral DNA and loss of HBsAg for at least 24 weeks after stopping treatment. After 72 weeks of follow-up, researchers found that: around 20% of patients receiving bepirovirsen achieved a functional cure no patients receiving placebo met the functional cure endpoint patients with lower baseline HBsAg levels appeared even more likely to respond, with functional cure rates reaching approximately 26% in some subgroups. Speaking to Medical News Today , study author Seng-Gee Lim , MD, Director of Hepatology at the National University Health System, Singapore, emphasized the importance of the trial findings: “Functional cure rate of 19% in Hepatitis B patients with HBsAg level [under] 3,000 IU/ml and 26% in those with HBsAg [under] 1,000 IU/ml. This finding provides meaningful cure rates for the first time for hepatitis B patients” “These findings are encouraging because they show researchers are making real progress towards treatments that go beyond simply controlling hepatitis B,” Opel Baker , MBChB, DipOccMed, MRCGP, general practitioner at the Mayfield Clinic Brighton and Hove, in the United Kinhdom, who was not involved in the study, explained to MNT . “While the treatment won’t work for everyone, every successful clinical trial helps us understand the disease better and brings us closer to more effective, personalised therapies. It’s an exciting step forward in a field that has seen relatively little therapeutic innovation for many years,” said Baker. Why are these results important? Bepirovirsen belongs to a class of medicines called antisense oligonucleotides (ASOs) . Unlike existing antiviral therapies, which primarily block viral replication, antisense therapies target the virus’ messenger RNA. This reduces production of viral proteins, including HBsAg, which researchers believe may play an important role in suppressing the body’s immune response against the hepatitis B virus. Lowering HBsAg levels may allow the immune system to regain some of its ability to recognize and control infected liver cells. Earlier clinical studies demonstrated that bepirovirsen could substantially reduce HBsAg levels, although durable functional cures remained relatively uncommon. Experts have long considered a functional cure one of the most important goals in hepatitis B research. Researchers hoped larger phase 3 studies would determine whether these responses could be achieved more consistently. While current treatments can suppress viral replication, they rarely clear HBsAg. This means that people remain at risk of viral reactivation if treatment is stopped. These findings suggest that a finite treatment course could allow some individuals to discontinue therapy while maintaining viral control. “A functional cure would be a major breakthrough for people living with chronic hepatitis B,” Baker highlighted to MNT . “Although current antiviral treatments are very effective at suppressing the virus, they rarely remove the need for long-term monitoring or treatment. A functional cure could reduce the risk of serious complications such as cirrhosis and liver cancer, lessen the psychological burden of living with a chronic infection and improve patients’ quality of life.” – Opel Baker, MBChB, DipOccMed, MRCGP Similarly, Lim also highlighted the importance of achieving a functional cure on liver and psychological health. “Clinical studies and meta analyses show that loss of HBsAg as seen in functional cure is associated with even more benefits than just viral control with suppressing the virus — more significant reductions in liver cancer, liver failure and mortality,” the researcher told us. “Moreover, compliance with long term antiviral therapy is suboptimal, while functional cure means patients are off all therapy. Functional cure has also important patient related benefits such as loss of stigmatisation and improvement in psychological health,” he added. Further research still necessary While encouraging, the results do not represent a functional cure for all people living with chronic hepatitis B. The trials enrolled a specific group of patients, including those without cirrhosis and with relatively low HBsAg levels. As such, it remains unclear whether similar outcomes would be achieved in broader populations. Additionally, roughly four out of five participants did not achieve a functional cure, highlighting the continued need for improved therapies and combination treatment approaches. Bepirovirsen was also associated with more adverse events than placebo, although many were mild to moderate. Longer-term follow-up will also be necessary to determine how durable these responses remain over many years and whether a functional cure translates into lower rates of liver cancer and other complications. The researchers plan further investigations to determine whether combining bepirovirsen with immune-based treatments, including therapeutic vaccines and other novel agents, could further improve cure rates. Although additional research and regulatory review remain ahead, the latest results suggest a functional cure for chronic hepatitis B may be moving closer to routine clinical practice. “It’s important to remember that research is moving fast, and there is a genuine reason for optimism,” Baker noted. “Although these treatments are still being evaluated, advances like this offer hope that future therapies may provide better long-term outcomes. In the meantime, patients should continue taking any prescribed medication, attend regular monitoring appointments and speak to their healthcare team before making any changes to their treatment,” he added According to Lim, “bepirovirsen is the first step towards functional cure, and this is hope for those living with hepatitis B but better therapies will lead to higher cure rates in the future.”
